# BACKLOG — GeotechStaffEngineer, planlens, geotech-references

THE one list of open work across the three repos: the app, `planlens` and
`geotech-references`. Owner's rule, 2026-10-12: earlier sessions kept starting
narrower to-do lists, and whole plans dropped off. There is no other to-do
list:
- HANDOFF §0a-current names only this week's slice, by ID.
- Plans, design notes, TRACE_REVIEWs, smoke REVIEWs, field-feedback FINDINGS and `LIVE_TEST_QUEUE.md` keep the detail and the evidence.
- Every open item in those documents has a line here pointing back to it.

## How every session uses this file

1. **Starting work.**
   - Read the owner's answers on the **Backlog Desk** (https://claude.ai/artifact/9M7saxwayRQ6c7v1TeDjyQ: `ArtifactData` list of collection `responses`) and fold any new ones in here (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/README.md`).
   - Read the Monday drift canary's latest report (`RemoteTrigger` `list_runs` on the routine `geotech-drift-canary`, then `get_run_log` of the newest run) and fold any breakage or version past `webapp/version_guard.py`'s ceilings in here (INF-12, INF-03).
   - Then read §0 and the tier you are working in, and say which IDs you are taking.
2. **While working.** Anything new goes HERE the moment it is found, under its area with the next free ID: a bug, a gap, a follow-up, an unverified claim, an owner question, a fix list from a review or a field session. The next free ID is the highest number in use for that prefix in this file or in `BACKLOG_DONE.md`, plus one (`.venv/Scripts/python module_work/backlog_dashboard/sweep_done.py --next GM` prints it); never reuse one.
   - One line per item: what it is and why it matters, then `*Src:*` the document holding the detail.
   - A detailed write-up elsewhere is fine. A to-do list elsewhere is not.
   - **No edge-case chasing (owner, 2026-10-27).** A follow-up that a fix's builder, reviewer or check turns up becomes its own item only if it changes a value in the saved reports' outputs (or what a live run returns). Otherwise it is one "noted, not fixed" line in the parent item's Done entry: keep note of the issue; the fix is not critical. The owner: "We need to stop chasing edge cases... In 6 months, we'll probably have a whole new set of core LLMs, and those edge cases might be caput anyway." Report ingest starts no new floor follow-ups (the rule-based readers) until the model-first measurement is in (RI-87, RI-168). Later is a pool of ideas, not debt. Owner questions on a technical point (like the specific-gravity hydrometer question) are still welcome; record the answer, and code it only if it changes a result.
3. **Finishing an item.** Delete its line and add it to "Done since the last sweep" with the commit. If a §0 item changed, update HANDOFF §0a-current's slice too.
4. **Releasing.** Every item the release closed moves to Done. Raise or lower tiers for the next train. Then sweep: `.venv/Scripts/python module_work/backlog_dashboard/sweep_done.py --note "app master <sha>"` moves the whole Done list, text unchanged, to the top of `BACKLOG_DONE.md` under a dated heading. Sweep on demand too, whenever the Done list grows long.
5. **A full sweep** (on the owner's ask, or every few weeks): re-read the plan documents against this file, as on 2026-10-12. Triage the Later and Parked items into a file (keep, merge, close as covered with the commit, or ask the owner about a drop) and apply it with `module_work/backlog_dashboard/apply_triage.py`, as on 2026-10-20. Then run `sweep_done.py` and record the date below.

After any change to this file, rebuild and republish the Backlog Desk (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/build.py`, same URL). Every `[owner]` item needs a plain-English card in `module_work/backlog_dashboard/context/`; the guard test checks this.

`webapp/tests/test_backlog.py` (part of the gate) checks:
- the IDs are unique, and each has a tier;
- every ID cited here exists, open or closed (in Done or in `BACKLOG_DONE.md`); every ID cited in HANDOFF §0a-current is open;
- no closed ID is reused, and `BACKLOG_DONE.md` holds only swept entries;
- CLAUDE.md and HANDOFF still point here, and every `[owner]` item has a desk card.

**Key:**
- **Tiers:** **Now** = the current focus, named in §0. **Next** = the next train. **Later**. **Parked** = the owner decided to wait, or (2026-10-27, the owner's rule against chasing edge cases) likely too narrow to implement: kept as a note, not planned; each says why. RI-168 re-reads report ingest's Parked and Later items once the model-first numbers are in.
- **[owner]** = needs the owner: a decision, an action, data, or CfA.
- *was:* = the item's id in an older list: HANDOFF `A1`–`E6`, or "Where things stand" `0`–`3e`. That old text is in git history at 414525a.
- **Short names:**
  - `H` = `HANDOFF.md` (line numbers as of e329b22)
  - `FI` = `module_work/FUTURE_IDEAS.md`
  - `B5` = `module_work/review_eval_results/2026-10-08_foundry_5.33.0rc1/` (Foundry brief 5 TRACE_REVIEW and slices)
  - `LTQ` = `module_work/LIVE_TEST_QUEUE.md`
  - `RA` = `module_work/REVIEW_ARCHITECTURE.md`
  - `RH` = `module_work/REVIEW_HARNESS.md`
  - `HT` = `module_work/harness_theory/`
  - smoke reviews = `module_work/live_smoke/runs/<wave>/REVIEW.md` (gitignored)
- **Last full sweep:** 2026-10-12 (app master 414525a, planlens f92541d = v0.13.0, refs c53e587 = v1.4.1).
- **Last tidy:** 2026-10-27: the 296 Later items triaged against the owner's rule against chasing edge cases (three read-only helpers, one yardstick): 125 to Parked with a reason each, 170 kept in Later, INF-30 closed as already complete. Before that, 2026-10-20 (app master 116ddb1): Later and Parked triaged (`module_work/backlog_dashboard/triage_2026-10-20.json`: 15 merged, 6 closed as covered, 9 to the owner as OWN-69), then the Done list swept to `BACKLOG_DONE.md`.

- **OWN-68** [Later] [owner] **Lateral piles: from what undrained strength is a clay 'stiff'?** The soil-profile shortcut to the lateral pile tool picks Matlock's soft-clay p-y curve below 100 kPa and a stiff-clay curve from 100 kPa: the module's own number, now a caller setting (`stiff_clay_cu`) stated in `choices`. GEC 9 calls 250-500 psf (12-24 kPa) soft (p. 216) and 1,000-2,000 psf (48-96 kPa) stiff (p. 219); COM624P prints no boundary (its stiff-clay tables start at 47.9 kPa). So a 48-96 kPa clay, which GEC 9 calls stiff, gets the soft-clay curve by default. Options: (a) keep 100 kPa; (b) 48 kPa, GEC 9's lower bound for stiff (stiffer response for 48-100 kPa clays); (c) no default, ask. Recommendation: (b), as printed. *Src:* GM-217 build 2026-10-19

- **OWN-73** [Next] [owner] **Cantilever wall sliding on soil with cohesion: which rule by default?** GEC-6 (p. 100) gives friction for drained loading (Eq. 5-38) and adhesion cu times B for undrained loading (Eq. 5-39) and says they should not be combined; EN 1997-1 6.5.3(10) neglects c' for drained sliding; UFC 3-220-20 (Fig. 4-27, Sec. 4-5.1.3) adds friction and adhesion Ca over the effective base width. Since GM-212 the tool follows GEC-6 by default (drained where phi > 0, undrained where phi = 0) and offers UFC's rule. A 5 m wall on phi' 28, c' 10 kPa: 1.51 under the old two-thirds rule, 1.23 under GEC-6, 1.55 under UFC. Options: (a) GEC-6 as built, UFC an option; (b) UFC by default; (c) ask for the rule whenever the base has cohesion. Recommendation: (a). *Src:* GM-212 review 2026-10-20
- **OWN-79** [Later] [owner] **The step between Eq. 4-12 and UFC's charts** (GM-232): with your routing, a vertical wall with level ground takes Eq. 4-12, and a face battered by even 1 degree (or ground sloping by 1 degree) takes Fig. 4-12 or 4-13, whose level-vertical curve reads 1-5 % above Eq. 4-12 at delta/phi' 0.66 (5.30 against 5.06 at phi' 30), so Kp steps by about 2-7 % as theta or beta leaves 0. (a) accept the step, as UFC prints both (each result names its route and lists the other sources' values); (b) scale the chart value so the route is continuous (not as printed); (c) use the charts' level, vertical curve for vertical, level walls at delta/phi' 0.66 too. Recommendation (a). *Src:* GM-232 review 2026-10-20
- **OWN-82** [Next] [owner] **Passive wall friction when the user gives none: three tool defaults to confirm**: (a) `sheet_pile` under log-spiral gives 17 deg (Table 4-1's clean-sand row) to EVERY layer without cohesion, while UFC 3-220-20 Fig. 4-37 step 1 (p. 237) includes wall friction only for clean coarse-grained soil and ignores it in all other soils; (b) `sheet_pile` under Coulomb takes delta = 0, while EM 1110-2-2504 (p. 4-3) and the USS manual (pp. 23, 29) use the table's delta with Coulomb; (c) `soe` keeps Rankine as its default passive, while Caltrans pp. 4-16/4-28 say to avoid Rankine where wall friction develops (its own soldier-pile examples use Rankine with delta = 0). Each result states its default and gives the alternatives' numbers. Options per part: keep as is, follow the cited source, or ask the user every time. Recommendation: (a) 17 deg only for layers the user marks clean sand or gravel, else 0 and say so; (b) the table's delta, as EM 2504 and USS do; (c) keep Rankine. *Src:* GM-242 review 2026-10-20 **2026-10-20 (GM-253):** (d) the coefficient tool's 1948 chart (and Caltrans Eq. 4-12) now REFUSES a log-spiral Kp with no wall friction given, since Caltrans p. 4-29 begins "Given delta, beta, and phi" and prints no default; NHI-06-089 p. 10-17 gives passive delta = phi'/2 as "the maximum wall friction suggested for design", which could be the stated default instead; the UFC routes keep delta/phi' = 0.66 (UFC 3-220-20 p. 191 "a common assumption"). **2026-10-21 (GM-257 review):** the coefficient tool's Coulomb route likewise takes delta 0 when `delta_deg` is left out, with no printed basis; it is declared, not changed.
- **OWN-83** [Next] [owner] **Downdrag: a clay's alpha by default, UFC's chart or 1.0?**: since GM-255 a clay with no alpha given takes UFC 3-220-20's Table 6-16 / Fig. 6-14 with the Eq. 6-9 transition zone, which reproduces UFC's own drag example (App. B-5.4: 52 ft, 133 kips) but lowers the drag on firm to very stiff clay (a soft-over-stiff case 1,148 -> 789 kN); UFC p. 476 notes that a higher alpha is conservative for drag. Options: keep UFC's chart (now); alpha 1.0 for drag with the chart's value shown beside it; or ask the user every time. Recommendation: keep UFC's chart, which is UFC's method and its example, with the alpha-1.0 drag shown beside it in `choices`. *Src:* GM-255 review 2026-10-20
- **OWN-84** [Next] [owner] **Drilled shafts: rock at the base with no RQD given**: a rock layer's RQD defaults to 100, so a base in rock with no RQD and no `rock_base_conditions_met` is taken as meeting GEC-10 (2018) p. 10-22's conditions and gets Eq. 10-23 (2.5 qu, the larger base; a probe 89,722 kN against Hoek-Brown's 47,378 kN with GSI 45, m_i 10); the default is listed in `defaults_used`, but p. 10-22's conditions are a finding about the rock below the base, not a default. Options: keep (stated); refuse a rock base with no RQD and no conditions flag, naming RQD, `rock_base_conditions_met` and GSI/m_i; or take Hoek-Brown where GSI and m_i are given and the conditions are not stated. Recommendation: refuse and name the three, since the base can double on this one input. *Src:* GM-251 review 2026-10-20
- **OWN-85** [Later] [owner] **MSE walls: a backfill Cu below 4 with no tested F***: GEC-11 Vol I Table 3-6 note (i) (p. 3-17) accepts the empirical F* only for Table 3-1 fill with Cu >= 4: "For fill outside these limits, tests must be run." Since GM-256 the tool takes a tested F* (`F_star_tested`); with Cu below 4 and none given it still computes the empirical F* and flags the fill as outside note (i). Options: keep computing and flagging; or refuse and ask for `F_star_tested`. Recommendation: refuse, now that the tested input exists, since the manual says tests must be run. *Src:* GM-256 build 2026-10-21

## 2. Report ingest

First priority since 2026-10-21 (the owner), with GSE on Foundry. Parked from 5.25.0 to 2026-10-20; every number since is GPT-5.4 on Foundry. Code items were confirmed open at master 414525a by a code check on 2026-10-12. OWN-11 (report ingest on the geotech page) needs RI-02 and RI-03 first; on Foundry, RI-03's engine route is the one INF-27 builds.

- **RI-46** [Parked] **Per-increment records' printed N unchecked**: a record written per increment ('4/6"-6/6"-7/6"') keeps its printed N but is not checked against its drives (`n_from_drives` reads whole counts only); reading 'X/6"' as a count there lets the check run. The reconciler still treats these records as refusals through its own "/" test (reconciler.py ~742), so they are left out of the drive-count set and get the refusal tolerance. *Src:* RI-44 builder and review round 5 **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Log floor check for one blow-record spelling; no wrong value written, only an unchecked printed N. Floor work waits on model-first. **Parked 2026-10-29 (RI-168):** Today's log floor.
- **RI-49** [Parked] **The stack reader's residuals**: 2 wrong sample ids in 5,000 fuzz trials (a sampler code printed a line low plus a lost line or a box; no N from them); a mark in another column printed against a stack's lower lines opens a phantom duplicate sample (pre-existing); a DATA column with a prefixed id ("S-3" over 4/6/7) still groups by depth span and splits at 0.25 m a line (no wrong N; the stack reader could take it but that changes corpus DATA-column records); a stack taller than its drawn box does not get the box's interval; the fallback drops Ns master had (225 in one fuzz run, 168 of them right), coverage lost on the safe side. Added 2026-10-23 (RI-45/RI-48 reviews): a silent wrong N in one fuzz trial of 5,000, the same on master (no sampler code and a lost N line, so the next sample's number "7" is read as the N of [41, 40, 5], truth 45, no row; without an SS code the drives check does not apply); a "k / R" stack with nothing after the R (a refusal at seating, or a ring code whose drive line was lost) sends the whole column to the fallback, taking its other ids and Ns (14 records a seed; master read the R as a sample id); 37-39 Ns a seed left out against master (N60 lines that happened to equal N, paren Ns in stacks that do not read, which master placed right 8-19 times against 372-387 wrong). *Src:* RI-44 builder and reviews; RI-45/RI-48 reviews **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Stack reader residuals found in fuzz trials (a few in 5,000); floor hardening the model-first route likely makes moot. **Parked 2026-10-29 (RI-168):** Today's log floor (stack reader).
- **RI-51** [Parked] **N60 in the final record**: the floor notes an N60 and computes N from the drives, but a model that reads the N60 as its n (with a box and a note) still wins the slot (merged N 17, the floor's 13 beside it, the note calling 17 a corrected N); without evidence the floor's 13 stands. Needs LogReading.n to say "the field N, not N60 or (N1)60" and a structured place for the floor's N60 so the merge keeps it out of the slot. Spellings still read as N: "N (60% ER)", "N-VALUE (60%)", and an "N60 (BLOWS/6 IN)" column (through the drive-increment exception; it does get a floor_n_drives row). *Src:* RI-45/RI-48 review round 1, 2026-10-23 **Parked 2026-10-29 (RI-168):** The N60 slot fight is in today's floor-and-model merge; on model-first only the schema wording matters, and MF-1 shows no N60 misread.
- **RI-52** [Parked] **Recovery and N printed in other forms in a stacked column**: a recovery printed in feet and inches (1'-6") still reads as 1 %, silently; "12/18" in a REC (IN) column could be read as 67 % (recovered over driven) instead of going to the note; an N printed as blows over a distance ('80/9"') in the N place stays unassigned, so that printed refusal is not kept as the record's n_printed. All three were there before RI-48. *Src:* RI-45/RI-48 review of 146588d, 2026-10-23 **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Rare recovery and N spellings in stacked columns; floor parsing of uncommon forms, predates RI-48. **Parked 2026-10-29 (RI-168):** Today's log floor.
- **RI-53** [Parked] **Bound pass follow-ups**: (1) a wrong or off-by-one triage range now moves the parent's own logs into the bound document (its DIGGS then 'skipped' in the parent) and a log straddling the edge is split between parent and child; log scores do not show it (each row now names the record matched, so it can be seen); (2) the bound document inherits the parent's reader label for its pages, so a parent mislabel inside the range is no longer given a second chance by the child's own rules; (3) a held page keeps the vote's `agreed` flag and confidence for the old label (labels.json under-counts split pages; set agreed False where the held label differs from the vote); (4) the reconciler's `_check_counts` counts LOGS while `explorations_found` counts HOLES (a continuation sheet gives 'found 2' beside a stored 1 under fill/settle); (5) the cluster stage skips a report that already has a run file without comparing switches (warn when run.json's switches differ); (6) the held-count note's grammar ('testPitCount stay as the narrative gave them'). *Src:* RI-14 builder and reviews, 2026-10-23 (6) 2026-10-26 (wave 2's skipsbound copy arm): `narrative_skips_bound` is still unmeasured: wave 2's only bound range (R36 pages 67-72) lies outside the narrative's window and R13 and R16 had none (RI-113); the arm re-read R36's narrative on the same pages, and its one-field difference is repeat spread (the same input read twice: one field in 28, 16 QA partials, 17 twin-field refusals). Measure it on R21 (wave 3) or on reports seeded with bound ranges, only where a bound page falls inside the narrative's pages or search window, with repeats. **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Bundle of small bound-pass follow-ups (off-by-one triage ranges, flags, a count, a warning, grammar); each narrow. **Parked 2026-10-29 (RI-168):** Bound-pass details; each narrow. Part (4), logs counted instead of holes, is noted with RI-157.
- **RI-54** [Parked] **Measure `counts_from_logs` on real reads**: run the ingest stage on Foundry with `score_on_cluster(ingest_counts_from_logs="zeros")` (then "fill") in a fresh out_dir beside an "off" run, against the 8 narrative truths; offline with rules-built stand-ins zeros was right 6 of 6, fill 5 of 17, settle overruled a correct narrative 12 of 13 times. 'zeros' rests on narrative_glossary convention 1 ('none means 0'), still DRAFT, so the owner confirms that convention before it goes on. Needs model calls (with OWN-11's run). *Src:* RI-14, 2026-10-23 **2026-10-26 (wave 2's copy arms, private review `raw/wave2_part2/TRACE_REVIEW.md` section 4.2):** zeros and fill changed nothing on wave 2's six reports: they act only on a count the narrative left empty, and the narrative answered every count; on run 1's 13 saved records zeros would act on 1 and fill on 4. - **RI-103** [Next] **Strata as the narrative defines them, not the log's lines** (OWN-95, owner 2026-10-26; some firms say 'layer' for the same unit). **Background done 2026-10-26** (11 public reports from several kinds of preparer in four countries, 26 borings; private notes `raw/strata_conventions_2026-10-26.md`): every narrative unit boundary fell on a log line (0 of 26 borings without one), but 18 of 26 borings had lines inside a unit; 9 of 11 firms draw a line at every change of description (even within one USCS group), 2 draw lines only at their strata and write smaller changes as notes ('gray below 3.5 ft') with no line; none draws at USCS changes as a rule; 3 of 11 logs label every interval's unit (a stratum column, a geologic symbol, a model-layer number), 3 tag some intervals (FILL, TILL), 5 none; 5 legends say the lines are approximate and transitions may be gradual; line styles are rarely defined; units can repeat down a hole. Evidence thin outside the US. The rule: (1) the log reader records every drawn line as an interval boundary (depth, elevation, the description as printed, classification), and a change written inside an interval with no line ('wet below 7 ft', 'as above, but brown') as a depth-tagged note, not a new interval; never merges intervals while reading; line style and extent kept where printed; the answer sheets follow the same rule (open sheets R04, R21 first; a blind sheet, R34, corrected by a helper that reports only how many rows changed); (2) a stratum label goes on the record only where the log prints one, kept as printed with its source, and a label carried down from one printed above is marked as carried; (3) mapping each interval to the narrative's units is a separate model step, one boring at a time, each interval to one unit or none; unit boundaries snap to the log's lines, and a narrative boundary with no line is flagged; evidence in this order: the log's own labels, a per-boring unit-depth table or a profile figure, per-boring depths in the narrative's text, then site-wide depth ranges with a description match (marked as the model's judgement); model-facing, under model-first, measured before use, after RI-101's first live sheets.

### Markups and checks

- **DR-30** [Parked] **The markup check returns a corrected box for rings and rectangles only**; callout, note and highlight looks are not asked for one (asking would change how their area check is measured). Also: the correction count runs per marked copy, not per turn, so a later "try again" meets the stop note until a clean check resets it. Live (F03): two boxes cut the note they mark but passed, because the check tests the note's centre; when a box is confirmed but cuts the thing, return the box the look found. **Parked 2026-10-27** (likely too narrow to implement, under the owner's rule against chasing edge cases): Hardens the markup check for more look types and a case seen once (F03); better models likely make it moot.
- **DR-31** [Parked] **`annotate_document` and `open_document` count a copy's markups differently**: the tool said the copy holds 6 markups where `open_document` counted 12, because each label is its own annotation. ### The app split (Document Review leaves this repo)

- **INF-26** [Later] **Split Document Review from GeotechStaffEngineer** (owner 2026-10-20): Document Review becomes its own package, not on PyPI, a wheel pulled into Tiny Apps; GeotechStaffEngineer runs as its own app in Palantir Foundry. **Plan merged 2026-10-20** (`module_work/APP_SPLIT_PLAN.md`, commit `aaf93ef`): three packages of our own from this repository (working names: `de-docreview`, the Document Review app, marked so PyPI refuses it; `docreview-core`, what both apps run, public on PyPI like planlens; GSE on PyPI as today); 16 steps after a step 0, each merged and tested on its own, guarded by a test that builds the Document Review agent with every geotech package blocked and by fingerprints of both pages' agents. Decided: the geotech page leaves Tiny Apps (owner, desk 2026-10-20 08:33); the testers' agent moves unchanged, `lean` decided later by DR-05 (lead, the owner can overrule). Owner decisions: OWN-72 (names, Tiny Apps route, CfA questions 6a-6d), OWN-74 (shared code), OWN-75 (release first), OWN-76 (public or private), OWN-77 (figure helper and plotting code), OWN-78 (notice and SharePoint folder). Steps: INF-30 to INF-35. *Src:* owner 2026-10-20 **Owner 2026-10-20 (after the first Foundry run):** Document Review is Tiny Apps only; Foundry only if Tiny Apps fails after its six-month pilot, and not planned around. So the split stands: two hosts with different rules (Foundry: Claude, no SharePoint, its own download and storage limits; Tiny Apps: GPT, SharePoint, CfA's pins). Order (lead, same day): the host fixes from the Foundry run (INF-36 to INF-39) go into today's package, into the shell that step 10 moves to the core; steps 3-8 (INF-30, INF-31) go on; steps 9-16 (INF-32 to INF-35) wait until the Tiny Apps tester start is set and the Foundry wheel route is known (OWN-80), as each new package adds a release. **2026-10-21 (INF-48):** plan rewritten for OWN-74 and OWN-76. Document Review becomes private source in the GHE wrapper repo (no package, no wheel). A thin public core goes on PyPI (about 37,900 lines; the owner's three named parts about 21,100). The document tools that only wrap planlens move into planlens (PL-29). Each app keeps its own chat page (about 12,900 lines, copied once at step 13). Steps 1-8 stand (step 7 now prepares the copy); steps 9-16 are rewritten: 9 planlens (PL-29), 10-11 the core (INF-32), 12-13 its release and Document Review's private home (INF-33), 14 the wrapper (INF-34), 15-16 GSE lets go and notes (INF-35). Still gated: OWN-72 (the name before step 10; CfA's 6a before step 14), OWN-78 (before step 14), OWN-87 (before step 13).

- **REF-48** (done, 2026-10-31, geotech-references main 1f7cac5; reviewed independently): a short section outranked the capacity methods in reference search. The cause was not length (eight length priors tried, each lowered MRR and dropped a genuine 85-token answer from the top 5): when no section holds every query word the search falls back to OR, and BM25 put a section holding four of the six words above two holding five. The OR fallback's top 50 hits are now ordered by how many query concepts each holds (a word and its synonyms count once), BM25 breaking ties; AND-mode results never move (4,710 title queries checked). Gold set: auto R@5 41 -> 42 and MRR .559 -> .563, off/fill/rerank unchanged, no case leaves the top 5 in any mode; the CPT case rises from rank 4 to 2. Follow-up REF-53. Noted, not fixed: GEC-6 5.3.5 and Micropile 4.3.5 rank high at median length, so this does not touch them; GEC-5 8.10.2 has no section (REF-52).

- **RI-139** (pacing follow-ups): 2026-10-27 (merge of d3c5902 + review fix 55a083f). (c) each call's clock, size, attempt and call number live on its own `_Request`, never on the engine, and CostMeter takes the call number, so with `range_workers>1` no row carries another thread's call (a two-thread test fails on the old engine); range_labels reads each page's deployment off its own reply; the Foundry engine's cap lessons go on the call's own list. (a) a pacer hold before a request that never went out is a `not_sent` row counted in `paced_seconds`, not in calls (the harness's call_health skips it). (b) `paced_seconds` in earlier_attempts, all_attempts, results.json totals and the RESULTS failed-requests line, only when nonzero. (d) a retry-after of 0 takes the engine's ladder step (`RATE_LIMIT_LADDER`) for the retry and the next request's hold, never 0 and not the 60 s minute (that is for a rate limit naming no wait). (e) `is_rate_limit` reads a 429 status, the class name, or rate-limit words in the error's own words only (the message before an embedded body, the body's message/code/type), so an error echoing request text is not a rate limit; the Foundry 400 wording still is. (f) a running per-minute token count is not built (README).

Follow-ups RI-142, RI-143.

Every `per_call` row has `started` (UTC, to the millisecond) and `duration` (the attempt's own seconds, waits excluded); a failed request's row has `input_tokens` from the error or the engine's estimate marked `input_tokens_estimated` (never in the totals).
- **RI-127** (pace after a large request): 2026-10-27 (merge of 606ee2c, reviewed). `score_on_cluster(tokens_per_minute=)` / `Budgets.tokens_per_minute` (default None, nothing paced or sent differently): after a request estimated above half the budget comes back, the next request to that model waits out 60 s; a rate limit with no retry-after (including Foundry's 400-as-rate-limit, filed by `engine.error_kind` from the glue's RateLimitGaveUp wording) holds the model's next request a window; the Prompter ladder's waits are at least 60 s (worst case 540 s a request, 6 retries as before); `paced` on rows, `paced_seconds` in the cost block. Set it from OWN-100's answer. Follow-ups RI-139.
- **OWN-99** (ask the AI FDE for the models' rate limits): 2026-10-27, answered by the FDE: the AI FDE cannot read Foundry's configured limits (its tools do not show them and the proxy's errors do not state them): they are set per model, in tokens and requests per minute, by the Foundry platform administrators in the Resource Management app (AIP usage and limits); as the FDE understands it they are one pool across the Foundry instance, which admins can split by project or kind of work, so our transforms likely share it with other users and jobs (possibly the published Streamlit app's Claude calls); it knows of no per-request input cap apart from the context window (a request near the minute's budget passes only when the minute is almost unused); the proxy sometimes reports a rate limit as 400 Bad Request, not 429 (run 5's give-ups already read as rate limits: 'RateLimitGaveUp: ... 400 Client Error'); its throttle at 22.5 GPT-5.4 calls a minute instead of 45 gave the resume run 16 rate-limit retries and no give-ups. The FDE also said Haiku 5.5 is not imported into the workspace (only Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and Sol); the owner corrected this the same day: Haiku 5.5 is available on Foundry as `ri.language-model-service..language-model.anthropic-claude-5-5-haiku` (the owner's example transform declares it as a model input), so it most likely needs adding to the wave's transform as a model input, and the brief keeps Haiku describing (D2). The FDE offers a probe transform (a few small calls, recording any rate-limit headers the proxy returns) and a table of run 5's rate-limit events against request sizes (no model calls). Next: OWN-100 (the admin's numbers, Haiku's model input, the FDE's two offers); the brief's stop point 7 and D2 rewritten.
- **RI-138** (name the lab sheets-per-call setting; count a split lab reading once): 2026-10-27 (merge of 1a2dea3; lead read the diff). run.json's `switches` records `lab_sheets_per_call` and the RESULTS ingest block prints it (None as off); in one document's `items/`, a lab item saved both whole (`item_N.json`) and split (`item_N-perK.json`) is counted once, the file matching the run's recorded K (else the whole one), the other under `superseded_item_files` with a note under Calls by step.
- **RI-136** (a printed N of '-' round-tripped as DIFFERS): 2026-10-27 (merge of 36bace1; lead read the diff). `diggs_writer._printed_n_text` treats a printed N that is only a dash (any of the hyphen and dash characters) or blank as no printed N: it is not written to the 'N-Value (as printed)' column and the gate does not look for it, so it compares as the reader's None; '>50' and 'R' are still written and checked. A dash row with no drives and no number is now not written as an SPT test at all (no test was run).
- **RI-137** (the calc reader's unit token lacked superscripts and micro): 2026-10-27 (merge of 2aacfee; lead read the diff). `calc_reader._UNIT_TOKEN` accepts ², ³, µ (U+00B5) and μ (U+03BC) inside a unit, and mu may open one (the unit table has µg/kg, µS/cm); a ² or ³ never opens a unit, so a footnote marker after a number is not read as one. The unit table already folds m² = m^2 = m2. µm/s is read with its unit but has no SI conversion in the table.
- **RI-111** (Unicode dashes in units; the DIGGS read-back gate): 2026-10-27 (merge of 454d8da + review fix 8ba0c16). `model._spellings` turns U+2010-2013 and U+2212 into the ASCII hyphen before every unit lookup and the calc reader's unit token accepts them (only unit text reaches it; ranges and minus signs read as before). The read-back gate now also compares the 'N-Value (as printed)' text column, each column checked once and named in the diff; on the five saved wave-2 records the new gate finds 0 differences where the old one gave R13 two false ones. Not done: the 78 entries re-merged on the saved readings (private, offline). Follow-ups RI-136, RI-137.
- **RI-112** (done with RI-111, the same merge): see RI-111.
- **RI-114** (narrative: twin fields in a citation): 2026-10-27 (merge of 97b79b8; lead read the diff). `TWIN_FIELDS` pairs each typed twin of the answer schema with its parent, read off `NarrativeReading` by name (today `strataList`, `bearingCapacityValues`, `reportDateISO`); a twin's citation is kept under the parent's name, goes through the quote check and merges with the parent's own; a name in neither schema still refuses, as does a twin citing a page outside the narrative. Not done: the 119 refused citations re-merged on the saved readings (private; a local offline run).
- **RI-128** (RESULTS: the ingest switches; a superseded narrative file): 2026-10-27 (merge of 9a71ab4 + 08e9819; lead read the diff). The ingest block prints one line for each switch a run file records (`ingest_bound`, `counts_from_logs`, `narrative_skips_bound`) with how many reports ran under each value, MIXED when they differ and 'not recorded' for older run files; within a document's `items/`, only the narrative file matching the run's recorded `narrative_skips_bound` (the file name's `-skips-bound` key) is counted in calls by step, older ones go under `superseded_item_files` and a note beneath the table (newest by file time only when the switch is not recorded, and the note says so).
- **RI-129** (done with RI-128, the same merge): see RI-128.
- **RI-124** (keep every attempt's cost): 2026-10-26 (merge of 60d72a2 + review fix 62f0a63). A report read again carries `earlier_attempts` (chained from the run.json it replaces) and `all_attempts` in its run.json; `results.json`'s ingest cost and RESULTS' Cost block count every attempt, with the last attempt's own figures beside them; reports left unfinished (error, token brake, give-up, skipped at the limit) are now all under failures and their spend is a Cost line of its own (`cost_unfinished`, `cost_every_report`); the per-report token brake counts every attempt and a report already at its limit is skipped while the run goes on; nothing sent to a model changed. The brief's stop points 3 and 8 now read these. Follow-ups RI-133 (a wave's ceiling across builds), RI-134 (an interrupted attempt's spend).
- **RI-121** (model-first page groups under the request limit): 2026-10-26 (merge of c730074 + review fix d518a62). `Budgets.max_request_tokens_in` reaches every model-first reader (bound reports' too); each page group's request is estimated before it is sent (`report_ingest/engine.py`: a PDF page as its text at 3.5 characters a token plus 4,784 tokens for its picture, a picture by its pixels, never under 1,700, the answer schema counted) and a group over the limit is cut with `_halves` (a log only within its own pages, a lab window at a sheet start where one falls in its middle half), a one-page group sent with a warning; each cut is an `over_limit` row on the item and a QA note on the record, and the run's note says a group was cut only when one was; today's log reader now counts its system prompt and answer schema (about 6.1 k tokens) against the limit, and lists pages the model named after a size stop as never read. Default None byte-identical (off snapshot unchanged); a 20,000-case random probe of the split found no page lost or read twice. Calibrate the 4,784-token PDF page from the first wave's `estimated_tokens_in` against `input_tokens`; set the limit from OWN-99.
- **RI-110** (a give-up must not lose an item silently; large log conversations): 2026-10-26 (merge of e1edbbb + review fix ec5e42d). A report with an item skipped for error ('could not be read') carries `item_errors` in its run.json, counts as failed for the stop rule, is kept out of the finished reports in RESULTS (failures line only) and is retried on resume with its read items kept; every ingest call's RESULTS covers every finished report in the folder; `max_request_tokens_in` (off by default) drops earlier pictures and then middle turns of a log conversation and splits a page window that does not fit, carrying cut pages to the next call and warning on any page never read. The R28 pages 76-87 re-run came back in wave 2's second hand-back (2026-10-26): read in 3 calls, 0 failed, but the pages hold seven boring logs and the reader returned one (RI-117); that hand-back also showed the limit was per minute, not per request (OWN-99, RI-127), and that a retry keeps only the last attempt's cost (RI-124). The model-first readers are not sized (RI-121).

A look (zoom turn) is counted apart from answers (one look per stage), so a first-call zoom no longer spends the continuation; the fold drops the first call's 'pages not shown' notes for pages the continuation read; each call's answer is kept beside the item (`answers`). Cited 0-based positions inside the item map to report pages under `refuse` too (page and box kept), a page outside the item still refuses, and the numbering is decided from the values' cites only. The shared mapping lives in `report_ingest/checks.py`, so today's reader loads no model-first module.

The summary-and-sheets code check counts a test the summary does not name with one it does when it is the same kind, on the same sheet or the page after, and names no other specimen (the lab reader's own same-specimen rule), so several runs on one sheet and one test over several pages no longer give findings; a run naming another sample still does.

- **INF-53** (the app installs and runs on Foundry's numpy 1.26): 2026-10-26 (squash 74c9cc3, reviewed twice). Cause: the platform pins numpy <2 through pyspark, and pip put scipy 1.18.1 (numpy >=2) on top, whose own np.long broke scipy's optimize, interpolate, integrate and stats; 'SALib is not installed' was pandas 3 failing to import. `constraints/foundry-numpy126.txt` (numpy 1.26.4, scipy <=1.17.1, pandas <3 for SALib 1.5.1, contourpy, opencv-python-headless, SALib, liquepy, pygef at their newest numpy-1.26 releases) with `constraints/foundry_requirements.py`; `docs/FOUNDRY.md` §1 item 4. np.trapezoid through `geotech_common/numpy_compat.py` (15 sites; a guard test scans for names numpy 1.26 lacks or 2.x removed). PyNiteFEA has no numpy-1.26 release: pynite is listed only where present (find_spec, no import at load), the structural specialist is told when it is absent, salib offers only modules present. numpy floor >=1.26 (a fresh resolve without the constraints still gives numpy 2). CI job 'numpy 1.26 (Foundry)'. Checked with no model calls: 16 of the 17 failed questions identified, their 20 tool calls run with answer-key values in tolerance; the July eval's 106 tool calls replay with 0 numpy errors (was 25 in 15 questions), numbers as on numpy 2. planlens floor lowered on its branch (6ae6441). The eval itself reruns on Foundry with the next wheel (INF-40).

B sent 50-56 pages to the wrong reader on the 13 stage-b reports against 182 and 290 (in sample). Carried out in RI-104.

The eval therefore runs on numpy 1.26: INF-53 makes the app install and run consistently there, then reruns the eval (owner, desk 2026-10-26: 'resolved with the FDE reply').

Unset, the library is the conversation's own `report_ingest` folder. `library_available` keeps the tool and its prompt line off until a record exists; never on the Document Review page or the reviewer specialists. Where the folder lives is the deployment's choice (the Foundry project, as with OWN-97). The library is checked when the agent is built, so a conversation's first ingest shows up on the next build.

- **INF-49** (pystra 2.0): 2026-10-23 pystra 2.0 replaced the whole analysis API, not only `Transformation.jacobian` (now `jacobian_u_wrt_x`): `Form`/`Sorm`/`AnalysisOptions`/`CrudeMonteCarlo(options, ...)` became `FORM`/`SORM`/`FORMOptions`/`SimulationOptions`/`CrudeMonteCarlo(sm, lsf, options=, rng=)` returning result objects, and `addVariable`/`setCorrelation` became `add_variable`/`set_correlation` (read from the installed 2.0.0 package, `pystra/migrate/_data.py`). `pystra_agent` now runs FORM, SORM and Monte Carlo through one helper (`pystra_utils.run_form`, `run_monte_carlo`) on either version; the `<2` cap is lifted. On a mixed lognormal/Gumbel/constant/normal problem FORM beta, pf, alpha and design point agree between 1.6.0 and 2.0.0 to about 1e-15, SORM Breitung beta to about 2e-9; Monte Carlo draws differ between versions (2.x ignores numpy's global generator; it is seeded from it so `np.random.seed` still repeats a run). Two fixes of old faults: a uniform variable was built as `Uniform(name, a, b)`, which pystra reads as mean a and std b on both versions (a uniform on [2, 6] had mean 2), now built on its bounds; SORM mapped alpha and the design point onto all variables including constants, now onto the random ones as FORM did. 363 tests pass under 1.6.0 and under 2.0.0. foundry/ and foundry_test_harness/ use none of the removed names. Commits `070bf7f`, `f656d5b`, merge `9f1b394`.

- **GM-228** (downdrag: warn when the UFC and GEC-12 depths below the toe disagree, owner OWN-62): 2026-10-20 every run computes both rules, UFC 3-220-20 Sec. 6-5.8.4.3 (p. 449: down to where the added stress is less than 10 % of the equivalent footing's bearing stress; the default) and FHWA GEC-12 Vol I Sec. 7.3.5.3 step 1c (p. 324: less than 10 % of the existing vertical effective stress); `below_toe["other_criterion"]` holds the other rule's whole run and `rules_disagree` says whether it warned; the warning fires when the pile head settlements or drag forces differ by more than `influence_depth_warning_percent` (default 25 % of the larger, or of `allowable_settlement` / `structural_capacity` when given) or when a settlement or structural check passes under one rule and fails under the other; neither manual prints a tolerance between the two (UFC p. 438, GEC-12 p. 309 checked), so `choices` says the 25 % is the engineer's to set; the warning names both rules with section and page, both values and depths, which gives more settlement, and that the choice is the engineer's; a profile that ends above a rule's depth is named with the depth to extend it to; the other rule failing leaves the result whole with the error noted; the calc package prints the same in plain words. Hand-checked: toe 2 m into sand 12.78 against 17.40 mm (26 %, warns); deep clay 885 mm by both (the head follows the soil at the neutral plane). Headline numbers unchanged. Commit `66fafe0` (c60294f, review 37b4969).

Commit `b4b9a08` (091e023, review a8c1224 / 85efc36).
- **GM-226** (judgment calls on by default, owner OWN-65: "just ship with it on regardless"): 2026-10-20 `GEOTECH_JUDGMENT_CALLS` is on when unset (`=0`, false, no or off turns it off; an empty value counts as unset); geotech answers end with their judgment calls, each with its source, after one references look-up (about $0.20-0.53 a turn); the chat control the owner named is Behavior's "Consult references": off, no references helper is built, the prompt asks for no consult, each call cites a `choices` basis or says it rests on judgment, and the turn-end check makes no extra model call; "Reference consult budget" caps the look-up at the smaller of the user's number and 6; both controls' help text says so and that it lowers cost; every geotech turn by default; the review page is unchanged; review_eval's `baseline` arm is now the page as shipped, with `judgment_calls_off` for the old one; the live-smoke flows that measured OFF now set it. - **GM-229** (driven piles, the owner's five GEC-12 readings, OWN-63): 2026-10-20 the alpha method (GEC-12 2016 Sec. 7.2.1.3.2, Steps 1-2, p. 250) reads Ca off Fig. 7-17 as drawn, with no limit on Ca, so alpha reaches about 1.1 (the D = 40b curve starts at su 1.0 ksf with Ca 1.1); API RP 2A's alpha of at most 1.0 (p. 252) applies only on the API route (Sec. 7.2.1.3.3; the 1.0 in Eqs. 7-13/7-14, p. 251, only picks between its two expressions); past the end of the Fig. 7-17 curves the new input `highly_overconsolidated_su_ratio` sends layers with su/sigma'vo at or above it to API alpha and holds Ca at the curve's end below it (GEC-12 prints no number for "highly overconsolidated", pp. 247, 250), `chart_end` and `api` stay as single rules, and a run with clay past the curves and no line is refused, listing every layer's su/sigma'vo, only where the line would change the answer; `choices` states the side each layer fell and, unchanged, Nordlund Step 8 (p. 244), the H-pile corrugated curve (p. 247) and D measured from the ground surface (p. 246). - **GM-07** (theta = 0.5 rings at drained boundaries; cloud session): 2026-10-20 the monolithic consolidation scheme with theta < 1 starts with Rannacher steps: each of the first `startup_steps` (default 2) steps is taken as two backward-Euler half-steps (Giles and Carter's recommendation); `startup_steps=0` restores the old stepping; the default theta is 1.0, so default runs do not change. V-023 at theta 0.5, 160 steps: excess pore pressure in the top metre over steps 1-5 -72.7/+78.6 kPa -> 4.8/25.2 kPa; base p/p0 at Tv 0.05 0.9950 against Terzaghi's 0.9969; the same to 4 decimals from Tv 0.2 to 2.0; final settlement 2.596 mm both. Commit `73da7b4` (review 62bc086 / afe167b).
